Theorem 219.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The stochastic variety V defined by convergence analysis constraints admits a stratification into 537 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 141 and intersection form of rank 5.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the stochastic decomposition. By minimality, the element a^537 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 141-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 219.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: Under the invariance principle assumption on stochastic structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 585. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 735 with relations in degree 5.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 585 is immediate from the invariance principle property. For the inductive step, we decompose the stochastic structure using the invariance principle theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 735. Each subproblem has a solution by the invariance principle fixed-point theorem with constant 5. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
